                       FOREWORD

    This is the official registry of all major launch operations conducted from Vandenberg Air Force Base,
    California. The vehicles are presented in chronofogical order beginning with the first launch on 16 December
    1958. Launch dates reflect Vandenberg local time.

    The information is further grouped into two matrixes titled: "ANNUAL LAUNCH SUMMARY BY BOOSTER"
    and "ANNUAL LAUNCH SUMMARY BY COMMAND." Two additional items contained in this document are a
    "LAUNCH FACILITY GUIDE" and a ''GLOSSARY."

    In the main chronology section, the two columns reporting cumulative numbers by booster and command
    represent only Vandenberg operations. They do not include, for instance, Titan IV launches conducted on the
    East Coast at Cape Canaveral, Florida.

    The 30th Space Wing History Office (30th SW/HO) periodically updates the Vandenberg AFB Launch

    Summary book. Questions may be directed to the undersigned at DSN 275-6421 or commercially at (805)

    605-6421.                    ·

    Jl:FFREY GEIGER
    Office of History

 ABRES      GLOSSARY
 ABRV
 ACC        Advanced Ballistic Reentry System
 ACE        Advanced Ballistic Reentry Vehicle (Formerly LBRV)
 ADC        Air Combat Command (Strategic Air Command [SAC] prior to 1 June 1992.)
 AE         Advanced Control Experiment
 AEC        Aerospace Defense Command
 AFMC       Atmosphere Explorer
 AFSC       Atomic Energy Commission
 AFSPC      Air Force Materiel Command (Air Force Systems Command [AFSC] prior to 1 July 1992.)
 AMARV      Air Force Systems Command (Air Research & Development Command [ARDC] prior to 1 Apr 1961.)

 ANT        Advanced Maneuverable Reentry Vehicle

            Army Special Test Program

 BATSAT

 COBE       Ballistic Missile Reentry System

            Cosmic Ray Observation Satellite

1The Pegasus flight program began with three "captive" flights using inert vehicles. These missions were conducted on 9 Nov 89, 15 Dec 89, and 30 Jan 90.
 The first seven "live" Pegasus flights, beginning on 5 April 90, as well as the inert flights, originated from the Ames/Dryden Flight Research facility at Edwards
 AFB, California. They were carried alof.t by a modified 8-52 aircraft provided by NASA. Beginning with the eight mission, Pegasus was carried by a modified
 Lockheed L-1011 aircraft and was staged from Vandenberg AFB.

2The Delta II program was operated by McDonnell Douglas Aerospace until the company was purchased by Boeing Aerospace, 4 August 1997.

--                                                                                   GLOSSARY---------

    SOFT - Missile emplaced and launched from exposed launcher above ground (e.g., Gantry-type; er Test Pad).
    SEMI-HARD - Missile emplaced in protective ground-level concrete housing prior to launch (see Coffin Type below).
    HARD - Missile emplaced in protective concrete housing underground prior to launch (see Silo and Silo-Lift below).
    SILO - Protective vertical tube-type housing from which missile is launched from its emplaced position (Minuteman and Titan II).
    SILO-LIFT - Missile is stored in silo, but is elevated clear of the tube for above ground launching (Atlas F and Titan I).
    COFFIN-TYPE - Completely enclosed horizontal storage from which missile is raised to exposed vertical launch position (Atlas D and E).
    STRIPPED - All recoverable materiel sold and removed under civilian contract, or retained by USAF but transferred to other activities.

    AAM     Air-to-Air Missile                                PALC       Point Arguello Launch Complex
            Advanced Ballistic Reentry System                 PLC        Probe Launch Complex
    ABRES   Ballistic Missile Defense Test Target Program     RAF        Royal Air Force (United Kingdom)
    BMDTTP  Ballistic Missile Reentry System (AB~ES)          R&D        Research and Development
    BMRS    Conunand Data Buffer                              RSLP       Reentry Systems Launch Program
    COB     Combat Training Launch (RAF/Thor)                 SAM        Surface-to-Air Missile
    CTL     Demonstration and Shakedown Operations            SLC        Space Launch Complex
    DASO    Intercontinental Ballistic Missile                           Special Operational Test (Titan II)
    ICBM    Improved Launch Control System                    SOT        Silo Launch Test Facility
    ILCS    Intermediate Range Ballistic Missile                         Software Status Authentication System
    IRBM    Integrated Weapon System Training                 SLTF
    IWST    Launch Emplacement                                           Test Pad (Peacekeeper/MX Missile)
    LE      Launch Facility                                   SSAS       Minuteman I Weapon System (MM IA and IB missiles)
    LF      Minuteman ICBM (I-II-III)                                    Minuteman II Weapon System (MM IIIG missiles only)
            Manned Orbiting Laboratory                        TP         Modernized Minuteman Weapon System (MM IIF and IIIG missiles)
    MM      Operational System Test Facility                  WS-133A    NUJ11erical identifiers for operational Minuteman wings:
            Operational Test (Launch Programs)                WS-133B
    MOL                                                       WS-133AM       I 341st SMW II 44th SMW III 91st SMW
    OSTF.                                                     Wing I-VI     IV= 351st SMW V = 90th SMW VI= 321st SMW
